From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n12\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.9, F33.9\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. I'm Dr. [Name]. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, nice to meet you, Doc. I guess I came in because... well, most days I wake up with this heavy, hollow feeling right here in my chest. It's just... it's like the old Ruben who could talk to anybody and actually enjoy his day is just completely gone. \n\nI used to be able to strike up a conversation with a brick wall. I'm in sales, you see, service contracts. Been doing it pretty much since high school. I remember this one client, Artie, he ran a diner right off the interstate. Artie used to make this incredible meatloaf on Thursdays. I'd always time my route so I'd hit his place right at noon. He sold the place a few years back, though. I think it's a mattress store now. I never really understood how those mattress places stay in business, you know? There's never anyone parked out front, and how often do people really buy a mattress? Maybe every ten years? My wife and I bought ours at Sears before they all closed down... \n\nBut anyway, yeah, I just... I don't know. I just don't have it in me anymore. That guy who could just walk in and enjoy the day, he's just missing. It's just that heavy feeling now.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that things that used to bring you joy or pleasure, like your sales job or visiting clients, don’t seem to anymore?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's... it's exactly like that. Not just the job, either. It's everything. I used to really look forward to bowling, you know? I was in a Tuesday night league down at Crestview Lanes. Crestview used to have the best shoe rentals, actually comfortable, not like those stiff plastic ones you get at the chain alleys now. Though I heard they're tearing Crestview down to build condos. Everything's condos these days. Where are all these people coming from to fill up all these condos? It just doesn't make sense to me, the roads can't handle the traffic as it is over on Route 9... \n\nBut, um, what was I saying? Right, things I used to like. I don't go bowling anymore. Same with working out in the garden, or taking my wife to those gallery shows she likes, or even going dancing. I just... I just sit there now. She'll ask if I want to do something, and I just make up excuses. It's just that none of it feels worth the effort anymore. Even the garden... we had these beautiful tomato plants a couple years back, beefsteaks, huge ones, but I just... I can't bring myself to care.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling guilty or blaming yourself for things more than usual, even when it's not really your fault?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I mean, I look at my wife and I just... I keep telling myself I'm useless now. Like all those years of taking care of my clients, providing for my family, it just doesn't even count anymore because I can't even handle this job now. It's like I'm a burden. \n\nAnd speaking of the job, they changed our expense reporting software last month. Used to be you just mailed in your receipts on Friday, simple, right? Now it's this whole app on the phone, you have to take pictures of everything. My phone camera is always blurry anyway. I think I dropped it in the driveway back in October when I was trying to rake leaves. The oak tree out front drops a million leaves, I swear. We should have never planted an oak. The guy at the nursery, Steve, he said it would be a nice shade tree. Steve retired, though. Moved to Florida, I think. Sarasota, maybe? Or Naples. A lot of people go to Naples... \n\nBut yeah, I just... I feel like it's all my fault that I can't keep up. I'm just letting everyone down.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you feel more tired or have less energy than usual, even if you haven't been doing anything physically demanding?\n\nPatient: Tired? No, I wouldn't say I'm physically tired. I haven't really noticed being low on energy like that. I still get up right at 6 AM the second the alarm goes off. I've had the same alarm clock for probably thirty years, actually. It's one of those old Sony Dream Machines with the glowing green numbers. It even has a cassette player built into the top. Not that you can even buy cassettes anymore. \n\nMy son found a milk crate full of my old ones in the garage last summer. Lots of Bob Seger, some Eagles... back when music was actually people playing real instruments, you know? Now it's all just computers making the sounds. I really don't know how anyone listens to it. My neighbor's kid, he drives this little Honda Civic and blasts that stuff so loud it rattles our front windows when he pulls in. I thought about going over and talking to his parents about it, but they just moved in a few months ago. From Arizona, I think. They had those license plates with the desert scene on them. I've always wanted to see the Grand Canyon, but my wife hates flying... \n\nBut, uh... what did you ask me? Right, energy. No, I'm not really tired or anything. Just... just that heavy feeling I told you about.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a loss of interest or pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy, such as your sales job, bowling, gardening, or going to gallery shows with your wife?\n\nPatient: Wait, didn't I... didn't we just talk about that a few minutes ago? I thought I just told you about the bowling and the garden and all that. Are you writing this down twice? \n\nYou know, my old manager at the firm, Gary, he used to make us write out our daily call logs in duplicate. We had those old carbon copy pads. You had to press down so hard with your pen just to get it to show up on the yellow sheet underneath. I always used those blue Bic pens, the ones with the clear plastic so you could see how much ink was left. They really don't make them like they used to, though. They always leak now. I actually ruined a good dress shirt just a couple months ago because a pen leaked right in the breast pocket. My wife tried everything to get the stain out. Hairspray, club soda... club soda is supposed to be the magic trick for everything, right? At least that's what the bartender at the old Elk's Lodge used to say. They closed that lodge down, though. Shame. \n\nBut anyway... yeah, like I said before, I just don't have any interest in any of that stuff anymore. None of it. Did I... did I not explain it right the first time?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight lately—like eating a lot more or a lot less than usual?\n\nPatient: Appetite? Weight? No, I don't think so. I mean, my clothes still fit the same. I've been wearing the same suit size for... oh, I don't know, fifteen years maybe? It's a 42 regular. I always buy them from this one place downtown, Miller's Menswear. Well, it used to be downtown. They moved out to that new strip mall by the highway a few years back. The rent was getting too high, I guess. It's ridiculous what they charge for commercial space these days. \n\nMy brother-in-law, he owns a little hardware store in Ohio, and he was telling me his landlord tried to raise the rent by thirty percent this year. Thirty percent! Can you believe that? Who can afford that kind of increase? He sells mostly specialty tools, stuff you can't just find at Home Depot. He's got this whole section just for woodworking planes. Really beautiful craftsmanship on some of them, brass fittings and cherry wood handles. I tried my hand at woodworking once, back in my thirties. Bought a lathe and everything. I was going to make a set of matching bowls for my wife's birthday, but I could never get the chisels sharp enough. You really need a good sharpening stone, one of those Japanese water stones, you know? \n\nBut, uh... wait, what were we talking about? Food? Yeah, no, I'm eating about the same. Haven't noticed any changes there.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly than usual, or maybe feeling restless and unable to sit still for very long?\n\nPatient: Moving slower? No, not really. I mean, I still walk the same pace I always have. I'm not running any marathons, but I'm not dragging my feet either. Restless? No, I sit still just fine. I actually spend a lot of time just sitting in my recliner. It's a La-Z-Boy, one of the really plush ones. Navy blue microfiber. \n\nWe bought it back when they had that big Labor Day sale at the furniture place on Route 9. You know the one, with the giant inflatable gorilla on the roof? I don't know why they always use those gorillas. What does a gorilla have to do with discounted sofas? It's like those car dealerships that have the inflatable tube men waving their arms around. There's a used car lot down the street from my office that has three of them. Red, white, and blue. They look ridiculous, especially when the wind picks up and they just flop over the power lines. I always worry they're going to cause a short circuit or something. \n\nMy cousin works for the electric company, actually. He drives one of those cherry picker trucks. Says the hardest part of the job is dealing with the squirrels. Apparently, they love chewing on the insulation around the transformers. He brought me this piece of wire once, completely stripped bare by squirrel teeth. Little menaces. My wife tried to put out a bird feeder last spring, and the squirrels completely destroyed it in two days. Chewed right through the plastic casing. We had to switch to one of those metal ones that spins around when they grab onto it. It's pretty funny to watch, actually. They just go flying off into the bushes... \n\nBut, what was the question? Moving slow? No, I'm fine. No changes there.\n\nDoctor: Would you say you’ve been feeling down or depressed most days, pretty much all day, even if it's not tied to anything specific happening?\n\nPatient: Yeah, yeah... I mean, that's why I came in, really. Most days I wake up with this heavy, hollow feeling in my chest. It's like the old Ruben—the guy who could talk to anybody, sell ice to an Eskimo, you know? Like he's just... gone. Just completely disappeared. \n\nIt reminds me of this guy I used to work with, Tommy. Tommy was the best salesman I ever met. He could walk into a room and just own it. He had this laugh, this booming laugh that made everyone else want to laugh with him. We used to go to this diner on 4th Street for lunch. Best meatloaf I've ever had. They served it with these real mashed potatoes, not the boxed flake stuff. You could always tell because there were still little lumps of potato in it. I hate the boxed kind. My mother used to make those when we were kids, and they always tasted like cardboard. \n\nAnyway, the diner owner, Gus, he was Greek. He used to tell us these stories about his village back in Greece, how they used to press olives by hand. He brought in this bottle of olive oil once, straight from his family's farm. It was so green, almost like lime juice. Tasted completely different from the stuff you buy at the supermarket. You know they cut that supermarket stuff with cheaper oils? Canola or soybean or whatever. It's a scam, really. You have to read the labels so carefully these days. Even the big brands do it. \n\nBut Tommy... yeah, Tommy just up and quit one day. Left the state. Never heard from him again. It's funny how people just vanish like that. But yeah, that hollow feeling... it's just there. Pretty much all day. Every day. It's just... heavy.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt this way before—like the old you was completely gone and you just couldn’t get interested in anything—for a period of two weeks or more?\n\nPatient: Have I felt this way before? Two weeks or more? No, this is... this is new. I mean, the losing interest part, that's what really gets me. I used to look forward to things, you know? Like my bowling league on Thursday nights. I've been on the same team for twelve years, the Pin Pals. Terrible name, I know, but we thought it was hilarious back then. We even got these matching shirts made at this little print shop downtown. \n\nThe guy who ran the print shop, Sal, he was a real character. Always had a cigar unlit in his mouth. He used this old screen-printing machine that looked like it belonged in a museum. He said the new digital ones just didn't have the same soul. I think he was right, honestly. The ink on those shirts held up for years. You don't see quality like that anymore. Everything is just fast fashion now, made cheap and meant to be thrown away. It's terrible for the environment, all those clothes ending up in landfills. My wife watches these documentaries about it. She's really into recycling and composting now. She bought this tumbling composter for the backyard. It's supposed to turn kitchen scraps into soil in like, a month. But you have to get the ratio of green to brown materials just right, otherwise it just smells like garbage. She had me saving all the dry leaves from the oak tree out front. \n\nThat oak tree is a menace, really. Drops acorns everywhere. I used to love working in the garden, keeping the lawn looking nice, but now... I don't know. Now I just look out the window at the leaves piling up and I don't even care. Same with taking my wife to those gallery shows she likes, or going dancing. We used to go dancing at this salsa club a couple of towns over. I was never very good, mostly just stepped on her toes, but it was fun. Now? Now I just sit in my chair and make up excuses. Tell her my knee hurts or I'm too tired. It just... none of it feels worth the effort anymore. It's like the color has drained out of everything.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in your life before this one where you felt down or lost interest in things for several weeks or more?\n\nPatient: Before this? Like, years ago or something? No, no, I don't think so. I've always been pretty steady. Even when the market crashed back in '08, and things got really tight at work... I sell commercial cleaning services, you know, contracts for office buildings and hospitals. When the economy tanked, everybody was cutting their budgets. Companies were firing their night crews, making the regular employees empty their own trash cans. Can you imagine? Paying an accountant a hundred grand a year and making him wipe down his own desk with a Clorox wipe. \n\nThose wipes, they smell terrible anyway. Artificial lemon. Like that lemon pledge my grandmother used to use on her dining room table. She had this beautiful mahogany table, claw feet, the whole nine yards. She was so protective of it. If you put a glass down without a coaster, she'd slap the back of your head so fast you wouldn't even see it coming. She had these coasters made of real cork, brought 'em back from a trip to Portugal in the seventies. Portugal is supposed to be beautiful. My wife's been talking about wanting to go there, take one of those river cruises down the Douro. They give you wine the whole time, supposedly. \n\nI'm not much of a wine guy myself. Gives me a headache. I prefer a good pilsner, something crisp. There used to be this little brewery down by the old railyard that made a fantastic pilsner, but they got bought out by one of the big conglomerates. Changed the recipe, started using cheaper hops. You can always taste the difference when they cut corners. It's like the olive oil I was telling you about. Everyone's just trying to save a buck. \n\nBut no... to answer your question... I've never felt like this before. It's completely new. And it's awful.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time in your life before this current period when you felt down, lost interest in things, or experienced a hollow feeling like you described, lasting for several weeks or longer?\n\nPatient: No, like I was saying, this is the first time. I've never had anything like this happen before. It's just... it's strange, you know? Being this age and suddenly feeling like a completely different person. I turned 58 back in October. My wife threw this surprise party for me at the house. She invited all these people from the neighborhood, even the new couple that moved in across the street. The Millers. \n\nThey seem nice enough, but he's got this dog, a Golden Retriever, that just barks at everything. A leaf blows across the lawn, it barks. The mailman comes, it barks. I tried talking to him about it once, the husband, I mean. He was out washing his car—a classic Mustang, cherry red, beautiful condition. He said he spent five years restoring it in his brother's garage. I always wanted to restore an old car, maybe a '67 Impala, but I don't have the patience for all that sanding and painting. The fumes give me a headache, too. Like when we had the kitchen cabinets repainted last spring. The contractor swore he was using low-VOC paint, but the house smelled like a chemical plant for a week. We had to keep all the windows open, and of course, it was unseasonably cold that week. The heating bill was astronomical. \n\nBut anyway, no. I've never felt this... this empty before. Never.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that during this current period, your low mood and loss of interest have been present nearly every day and have lasted for at least two weeks straight?\n\nPatient: Yeah, every day. It's been... gosh, about eight months now. Ever since that new regional manager came in. Guy's a piece of work, let me tell you. Started coming down on me for every little thing, taking away accounts I'd handled for years. Accounts I built from the ground up! Like the Henderson building downtown. I knew the facility manager there, Gary, since his kids were in diapers. Now they're off at college. His oldest went to Penn State, studying engineering or something. Smart kid. Gary was so proud. He used to bring these incredible homemade brownies into the office whenever I'd stop by to renew the contract. His wife's recipe. She uses dark chocolate and puts a little espresso powder in the batter, says it brings out the chocolate flavor. I tried telling my wife to do that, but she bought this cheap store-brand cocoa powder instead, and they just tasted like dust. \n\nYou really get what you pay for at the grocery store these days. Have you seen the price of eggs lately? It's highway robbery. The whole supply chain is messed up. I read this article about how there's a shortage of truck drivers causing all these delays. Makes you think about getting a CDL, you know? Just hit the open road. See the country. I always wanted to drive through the Southwest, see the Grand Canyon. My brother went a few years ago and took all these pictures, but he's terrible with a camera. Everything was blurry or he had his thumb over the lens. You'd think with these new smartphones it would be impossible to take a bad picture, but he manages. \n\nBut yeah, to answer your question... it's been every day for eight months. Just... completely drained.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Didn't we just... wait, didn't you just ask me that a few minutes ago? I feel like I'm losing my mind today, or I'm a broken record. But no, doc, like I said, never. I was always the steady guy. You have to be, in sales. You can't walk into a pitch meeting looking like somebody shot your dog. You gotta have the firm handshake, the eye contact, the big smile. 

My dad taught me that. He used to sell encyclopedias door-to-door back in the seventies. Britannica, mostly. Heavy books, too. Can you imagine doing that now? Just walking up to a stranger's house and ringing the bell? People would call the cops on you so fast. Everybody's terrified of their own shadow these days. I blame those doorbell cameras. My neighbor, the one with the barking dog I told you about, he put one in and now he watches his front porch like a hawk. He posts every little thing on that neighborhood app on his phone. The other day he was panicking over a "suspicious van" that turned out to be the guy coming to clean my gutters. 

You gotta clean them twice a year in our neighborhood because of all the oak trees. The leaves and acorns get stuck in the downspouts, and then the water backs up under the shingles. Roof damage is no joke. A new roof will run you fifteen, twenty grand easy right now with the cost of materials. Lumber went through the roof during the pandemic and it never really came back down. It's just like I was saying about the eggs. Everybody's using any excuse to jack up the prices. 

But... I'm sorry, what were we talking about? Oh, right. Feeling down before. No, I never had a period like this before in my life. Just this one, and I can't seem to shake it.
